The paths I can identify are:
1. H → I → O (direct path)
2. H → I → P → J → O
3. H → G → F → K → I → O
4. H → G → F → X → K → I → O
5. H → G → F → I → O
6. H → G → F → I → P → J → O

I observe that all paths between H and O must pass through either node I or node J:
- Path 1 goes through I
- Path 2 goes through both I and J
- Paths 3-6 all go through I, and some also go through J

To disconnect H and O, I need to break all possible paths. If I remove only I, path H → G → F → I → O would be broken, but path H → I → P → J → O would still exist (going through J). Similarly, if I remove only J, path H → I → P → J → O would be broken, but path H → I → O would still exist.

Therefore, I must remove both I and J to ensure no path exists between H and O. This is the minimum cut set, as removing fewer than two nodes cannot disconnect H and O.

Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [I, J]
